=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        wlen= len(words[0])
        slen= wlen*len(words)
        track=dict()
        
        occ = collections.Counter(words)

        def test():
            for key, val in track.items():
                if val !=occ[key]:
                    return False
            return True
        res=[]
        #first look

        for k in range(wlen):
            for i in words:
                track.update({i : 0})
            for i in range(k,slen+k,wlen):
                w=s[i:i+wlen]
                if w in words:
                    track.update({w: track[w]+1})
            if test():
                res.append(k)
            #complete
            for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1,wlen):
                
                nw=s[i+slen-wlen:i+slen]
                pw=s[i-wlen:i]
                if nw in words:
                    track.update({nw: track[nw]+1})
                if pw in words:
                    track.update({pw: track[pw]-1})
                if test():
                    res.append(i-wlen)
        return res

class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        wlen= len(words[0])
        slen= wlen*len(words)
        track=dict()
        
        occ = collections.Counter(words)

        def test():
            for key, val in track.items():
                if val !=occ[key]:
                    return False
            return True
        res=[]
        #first look

        for k in range(wlen):
            for i in words:
                track.update({i : 0})
            for i in range(k,slen+k,wlen):
                w=s[i:i+wlen]
                if w in words:
                    track.update({w: track[w]+1})
            if test():
                res.append(k)

            for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1,wlen):
                
                nw=s[i+slen-wlen:i+slen]
                pw=s[i-wlen:i]
                if nw in words:
                    track.update({nw: track[nw]+1})
                if pw in words:
                    track.update({pw: track[pw]-1})
                if test():
                    res.append(i-wlen)

- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.

- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:

```
class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        wlen= len(words[0])
        slen= wlen*len(words)
        track=dict()
        
        occ = collections.Counter(words)

        def test():
            for key, val in track.items():
                if val !=occ[key]:
                    return False
            return True
        res=[]
        #first look

        for k in range(wlen):
            for i in words:
                track.update({i : 0})
            for i in range(k,slen+k,wlen):
                w=s[i:i+wlen]
                if w in words:
                    track.update({w: track[w]+1})
            if test():
                res.append(k)
            #complete
            for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1,wlen):
                
                nw=s[i+slen-wlen:i+slen]
                pw=s[i-wlen:i]
                if nw in words:
                    track.update({nw: track[nw]+1})
                if pw in words:
                    track.update({pw: track[pw]-1})
                if test():
                    res.append(i-wlen)
        return res
```

Now, are there any other bugs? Let's inspect code line by line.

```
class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        wlen= len(words[0])
        slen= wlen*len(words)
        track=dict()
        
        occ = collections.Counter(words)

        def test():
            for key, val in track.items():
                if val !=occ[key]:
                    return False
            return True
        res=[]
        #first look

        for k in range(wlen):
            for i in words:
                track.update({i : 0})
            for i in range(k,slen+k,wlen):
                w=s[i:i+wlen]
                if w in words:
                    track.update({w: track[w]+1})
            if test():
                res.append(k)
            #complete
            for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1,wlen):
                
                nw=s[i+slen-wlen:i+slen]
                pw=s[i-wlen:i]
                if nw in words:
                    track.update({nw: track[nw]+1})
                if pw in words:
                    track.update({pw: track[pw]-1})
                if test():
                    res.append(i-wlen)
        return res
```

Good. But what if track has a key with val 0 and occ[key] 0? Not possible because track keys are words with positive occ. Unless words list empty. Not.

Then nw end = i+slen <= len(s). Good. pw start = i-wlen >=? For first i = wlen+k, pw start = k >=0. Good. So no out-of-range.

Potential issue: In initial loop, for k maybe > len(s)-slen, range(k, slen+k, wlen) can have i beyond len(s). Slicing okay. But `i+slen` not used. Good.

Potential issue: The code uses `track.update({w: track[w]+1})`; if `w` is a word but `track` doesn't have key? It does. But if `words` list has duplicates, `track` has key. Good.

Potential issue: `track` is a dict and `occ` Counter. In `test`, `for key, val in track.items()` while track may be mutated? No, test called after updates, no mutation inside. Good.

Then for next iteration i += wlen. Now current window before update starts at previous i = k+wlen. It should remove word at previous start (k+wlen) and add word at previous end (k+wlen+slen). But what about the last valid start with same offset that is less than len(s)-slen? It will be included because range stop is exclusive but includes values < stop. If last valid start = len(s)-slen, included if congruent and < stop. Good.

But wait, let's re-evaluate with a different interpretation: Maybe the loop variable `i` is intended to be the end of the previous window? Let's see. They set `for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1, wlen)`. If `i` were old start? Old start after initial would be k. But range starts at wlen+k, not k. If `i` were old end? Old end after initial is k+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not k+slen. So no. If `i` were new end? New end after first slide is k+wlen+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not. So i is new start. Append i.

Old end after initial is k+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not k+slen. So no. If `i` were new end? New end after first slide is k+wlen+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not. So i is new start. Append i.

But let's check the initial loop's `if test(): res.append(k)`. If k is start. Then slide loop's first i = k+wlen. If append i, result sorted within offset. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `track` is a dict, and `test()` uses `track.items()`. If `track` has a key with val 0 and occ[key] positive, false. But what if `track` has a key with val 0 and occ[key] positive, but another key has val > occ? false. Good.

The largest valid start <= len(s)-slen. The range stop is len(s)-slen+1, so it includes all values < stop. If the largest valid start is, say, len(s)-slen - r where r < wlen, it is included if congruent. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `for i in range(k,slen+k,wlen):` If `k` is not 0 and `slen+k` is not a multiple of wlen? slen is multiple of wlen, so slen+k mod wlen = k. Range step wlen, starts at k, so includes k, k+wlen, ..., k+slen-wlen. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1,wlen):` If `len(s)-slen+1` is not congruent to k mod wlen, range stops before. But all valid starts with same offset are k + m*wlen. The largest valid start <= len(s)-slen. The range stop is len(s)-slen+1, so it includes all values < stop. If the largest valid start is, say, len(s)-slen - r where r < wlen, it is included if congruent. Good.

But wait, let's re-evaluate with a different interpretation: Maybe the loop variable `i` is intended to be the end of the previous window? Let's see. Old end after initial is k+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not k+slen. So no. If `i` were new end? New end after first slide is k+wlen+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not. So i is new start. Append i.

But let's check the initial loop's `if test(): res.append(k)`. If k is start. Then slide loop's first i = k+wlen. If append i, result sorted within offset. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `track` is a dict, and `test()` uses `track.items()`. If `track` has a key with val 0 and occ[key] positive, false. But what if `track` has a key with val 0 and occ[key] positive, but another key has val > occ? false. Good.

The largest valid start <= len(s)-slen. The range stop is len(s)-slen+1, so it includes all values < stop. If the largest valid start is, say, len(s)-slen - r where r < wlen, it is included if congruent. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `for i in range(k,slen+k,wlen):` If `k` is not 0 and `slen+k` is not a multiple of wlen? slen is multiple of wlen, so slen+k mod wlen = k. Range step wlen, starts at k, so includes k, k+wlen, ..., k+slen-wlen. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1,wlen):` If `len(s)-slen+1` is not congruent to k mod wlen, range stops before. But all valid starts with same offset are k + m*wlen. The largest valid start <= len(s)-slen. The range stop is len(s)-slen+1, so it includes all values < stop. If the largest valid start is, say, len(s)-slen - r where r < wlen, it is included if congruent. Good.

But wait, let's re-evaluate with a different interpretation: Maybe the loop variable `i` is intended to be the end of the previous window? Let's see. Old end after initial is k+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not k+slen. So no. If `i` were new end? New end after first slide is k+wlen+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not. So i is new start. Append i.

But let's check the initial loop's `if test(): res.append(k)`. If k is start. Then slide loop's first i = k+wlen. If append i, result sorted within offset. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `track` is a dict, and `test()` uses `track.items()`. If `track` has a key with val 0 and occ[key] positive, false. But what if `track` has a key with val 0 and occ[key] positive, but another key has val > occ? false. Good.

The largest valid start <= len(s)-slen. The range stop is len(s)-slen+1, so it includes all values < stop. If the largest valid start is, say, len(s)-slen - r where r < wlen, it is included if congruent. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `for i in range(k,slen+k,wlen):` If `k` is not 0 and `slen+k` is not a multiple of wlen? slen is multiple of wlen, so slen+k mod wlen = k. Range step wlen, starts at k, so includes k, k+wlen, ..., k+slen-wlen. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1,wlen):` If `len(s)-slen+1` is not congruent to k mod wlen, range stops before. But all valid starts with same offset are k + m*wlen. The largest valid start <= len(s)-slen. The range stop is len(s)-slen+1, so it includes all values < stop. If the largest valid start is, say, len(s)-slen - r where r < wlen, it is included if congruent. Good.

But wait, let's re-evaluate with a different interpretation: Maybe the loop variable `i` is intended to be the end of the previous window? Let's see. Old end after initial is k+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not k+slen. So no. If `i` were new end? New end after first slide is k+wlen+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not. So i is new start. Append i.

But let's check the initial loop's `if test(): res.append(k)`. If k is start. Then slide loop's first i = k+wlen. If append i, result sorted within offset. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `track` is a dict, and `test()` uses `track.items()`. If `track` has a key with val 0 and occ[key] positive, false. But what if `track` has a key with val 0 and occ[key] positive, but another key has val > occ? false. Good.

The largest valid start <= len(s)-slen. The range stop is len(s)-slen+1, so it includes all values < stop. If the largest valid start is, say, len(s)-slen - r where r < wlen, it is included if congruent. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `for i in range(k,slen+k,wlen):` If `k` is not 0 and `slen+k` is not a multiple of wlen? slen is multiple of wlen, so slen+k mod wlen = k. Range step wlen, starts at k, so includes k, k+wlen, ..., k+slen-wlen. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1,wlen):` If `len(s)-slen+1` is not congruent to k mod wlen, range stops before. But all valid starts with same offset are k + m*wlen. The largest valid start <= len(s)-slen. The range stop is len(s)-slen+1, so it includes all values < stop. If the largest valid start is, say, len(s)-slen - r where r < wlen, it is included if congruent. Good.

But wait, let's re-evaluate with a different interpretation: Maybe the loop variable `i` is intended to be the end of the previous window? Let's see. Old end after initial is k+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not k+slen. So no. If `i` were new end? New end after first slide is k+wlen+slen. Range starts at wlen+k, not. So i is new start. Append i.

But let's check the initial loop's `if test(): res.append(k)`. If k is start. Then slide loop's first i = k+wlen. If append i, result sorted within offset. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `track` is a dict, and `test()` uses `track.items()`. If `track` has a key with val 0 and occ[key] positive, false. But what if `track` has a key with val 0 and occ[key] positive, but another key has val > occ? false. Good.

The largest valid start <= len(s)-slen. The range stop is len(s)-slen+1, so it includes all values < stop. If the largest valid start is, say, len(s)-slen - r where r < wlen, it is included if congruent. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `for i in range(k,slen+k,wlen):` If `k` is not 0 and `slen+k` is not a multiple of wlen? slen is multiple of wlen, so slen+k mod wlen = k. Range step wlen, starts at k, so includes k, k+wlen, ..., k+slen-wlen. Good.

Potential issue: The code's `for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1,wlen):` If `len(s)-slen+1` is not congruent to k mod wlen, range stops before. But all valid starts with same offset are k + m*wlen. The largest valid start <= len(s)-slen. The range stop is len(s)-slen+1, so it includes all values < stop. If the largest valid start is, say, len(s)-slen - r where r < wlen, it is included if congruent. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        wlen = len(words[0])
        slen = wlen * len(words)
        track = {}

        occ = collections.Counter(words)

        def test():
            for key, val in track.items():
                if val != occ[key]:
                    return False
            return True

        res = []

        for k in range(wlen):
            for word in words:
                track[word] = 0

            for i in range(k, slen + k, wlen):
                w = s[i:i + wlen]
                if w in words:
                    track[w] += 1

            if test():
                res.append(k)

            for i in range(wlen + k, len(s) - slen + 1, wlen):
                nw = s[i + slen - wlen:i + slen]
                pw = s[i - wlen:i]

                if nw in words:
                    track[nw] += 1
                if pw in words:
                    track[pw] -= 1

                if test():
                    res.append(i)

        return res
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        wlen = len(words[0])
        slen = wlen * len(words)
        track = {}

        occ = collections.Counter(words)

        def test():
            for key, val in track.items():
                if val != occ[key]:
                    return False
            return True

        res = []

        for k in range(wlen):
            for word in words:
                track[word] = 0

            for i in range(k, slen + k, wlen):
                w = s[i:i + wlen]
                if w in words:
                    track[w] += 1

            if test():
                res.append(k)

            for i in range(wlen + k, len(s) - slen + 1, wlen):
                nw = s[i + slen - wlen:i + slen]
                pw = s[i - wlen:i]

                if nw in words:
                    track[nw] += 1
                if pw in words:
                    track[pw] -= 1

                if test():
                    res.append(i)

        return res
